\displaystyle+\int b^{s}(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)\mathrm{1}_{\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq 2^{-\frac{k+l}{2}}}[({\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(\xi^{+})-({\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})](\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\overline{\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}})(\xi)\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\xi:=\mathcal{X}_{k,l,1}+\mathcal{X}_{k,l,2}.

Similarly, we have

|\mathcal{X}_{k,l,1}|\lesssim s^{-1}2^{sk+sl}|{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|_{L^{2}}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}|\mathcal{X}_{k,l,1}|\lesssim s^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{k,l}2^{2sl}|{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\sum_{k,l}2^{2sk}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}\lesssim s^{-1}|W_{s}(D)h|_{L^{2}}|W_{s}f|_{L^{2}}.

By Taylor expansion,

({\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(\xi^{+})-({\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})=(1-\frac{1}{\cos\theta})\int_{0}^{1}(\nabla(\varphi_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}))(\xi^{+}(\iota))\cdot\xi^{+}\mathrm{d}\iota,

where
\xi^{+}(\iota)=(1-\iota)|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|}+\iota\xi^{+}
,
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}_{k,l,2}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,1]\times\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{s}(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)(1-\frac{1}{\cos\theta})\mathrm{1}_{\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq 2^{-\frac{k+l}{2}}}(\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\overline{\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}})(\xi)(\nabla(\varphi_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(\xi^{+}(\iota)))\cdot\xi^{+}\mathrm{d}\iota\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\xi|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\int_{0}^{2^{-\frac{k+l}{2}}}\theta^{1-2s}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}(\xi)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\xi)^{1/2}(\int_{0}^{2^{-\frac{k+l}{2}}}\theta^{1-2s}|u|^{2}|\nabla(\varphi_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(u)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}u)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{(s-1)(k+l)}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|_{L^{2}}(\int|u|^{2}|\nabla(\varphi_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(u)|^{2}\mathrm{d}u)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{(s-1)(k+l)}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|_{L^{2}}(|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|_{L^{2}}+2^{l}|\varphi_{l}\widehat{v\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|_{L^{2}}),

where we have used the change of variable
\xi\rightarrow u=\xi^{+}(\iota)
and the fact
that

\displaystyle\int|u|^{2}|\nabla(\varphi_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h})(u)|^{2}\mathrm{d}u\ \lesssim|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2^{2l}|\varphi_{l}\widehat{v\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Since
2^{(s-1)(k+l)}\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}2^{(s-1)(k+l)}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|_{L^{2}}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|f|_{L^{2}}|h|_{L^{2}}.

It is straightforward to check that

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}2^{(s-1)(k+l)}2^{l}|\varphi_{l}\widehat{v\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|_{L^{2}}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k,l}2^{2sl}|\tilde{\varphi}_{l}\widehat{\varphi_{k}f}|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\sum_{k,l}2^{2sk-2k}|\varphi_{l}\widehat{v\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h}|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k}|W_{s}(D)\varphi_{k}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\sum_{k}2^{2sk-2k}|v\tilde{\varphi}_{k}h|^{2}_{L^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}\lesssim|W_{s}(D)f|_{L^{2}}|W_{s}h|_{L^{2}}.

Thus, we have

\sum_{k,l}|\mathcal{X}_{k,l,2}|\lesssim|W_{s}(D)f|_{L^{2}}|W_{s}h|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}(h,f)|\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)h|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)f|_{L^{2}}).

(3.4)

Step 2: With the term
|u|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(u)
.
We write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)=\mathcal{Y}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)+\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(h,f),

according to
\psi^{\eta}(u)=(1-\psi(u))+(\psi(u)-\psi_{\eta}(u))
.

Estimate of
\mathcal{Y}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)
containing
|u|^{\gamma}(1-\psi(u))
.

Set
H(u):=h\langle u\rangle^{-\gamma}|u|^{\gamma}(1-\psi(u))
and
w=|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|}
, then
W_{\gamma/2}(u)=W_{\gamma/2}(w)
and

\displaystyle\langle u\rangle^{\gamma}H(u)[f(u^{+})-f(w)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(W_{\gamma/2}H)(u)[(W_{\gamma/2}f)(u^{+})-(W_{\gamma/2}f)(w)]

\displaystyle+(W_{\gamma/2}H)(u)(W_{\gamma/2}f)(u^{+})(W_{\gamma/2}(w)W_{-\gamma/2}(u^{+})-1).

From this, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(W_{\gamma/2}H,W_{\gamma/2}f)+\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)(W_{\gamma/2}H)(u)

\displaystyle\times(W_{\gamma/2}f)(u^{+})(W_{\gamma/2}(w)W_{-\gamma/2}(u^{+})-1)\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u:=\mathcal{X}(W_{\gamma/2}H,W_{\gamma/2}f)+\mathcal{A}.

Thanks to (
3.4
) in
Step 1
and Lemma
2.1
with
M=W^{s}\in S^{s}_{1,0}
and
\Phi=\langle\cdot\rangle^{-\gamma}|\cdot|^{\gamma}(1-\psi(\cdot))\in S^{0}_{1,0}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}(W_{\gamma/2}H,W_{\gamma/2}f)|\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}).

By noting

|W_{\gamma/2}(u)W_{-\gamma/2}(u^{+})-1|\lesssim\theta^{2}

and using the change of variable
u\rightarrow u^{+}
,
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|(W_{\gamma/2}H)(u)|^{2}|W_{\gamma/2}(w)W_{-\gamma/2}(u^{+})-1|\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\big(\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|(W_{\gamma/2}f)(u^{+})|^{2}|W_{\gamma/2}(w)W_{-\gamma/2}(u^{+})-1|\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|W_{\gamma/2}H|_{L^{2}}|W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}|W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}.

Combining the estimates of
\mathcal{X}(W_{\gamma/2}H,W_{\gamma/2}f)
and
\mathcal{A}
gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Y}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)|\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}).

Estimate of
\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)
containing

|u|^{\gamma}(\psi(u)-\psi_{\eta}(u))
.

Since the support of
|u|^{\gamma}(\psi(u)-\psi_{\eta}(u))
belongs to
\frac{3}{4}\eta\leq|u|\leq\frac{4}{3}
, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)=\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)\tilde{W}(u)\tilde{H}(u)[\tilde{F}(u^{+})-\tilde{F}(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})]\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u,

(3.5)

where
\tilde{W}(u):=|u|^{\gamma}(\psi(u)-\psi_{\eta}(u)),\tilde{H}:=\psi_{2}h
,
\tilde{F}:=\psi_{2}f
. By (
3.4
) in
Step 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)|\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}\tilde{W}\tilde{H}|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)\tilde{W}\tilde{H}|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}\tilde{F}|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)\tilde{F}|_{L^{2}}).

By
|\tilde{W}(u)|\lesssim\psi(u)\eta^{\gamma}
, we have
|W_{s}\tilde{W}\tilde{H}|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}
.
Note that
|\tilde{W}|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma+\frac{3}{2}},|\tilde{W}|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma+\frac{1}{2}},|\tilde{W}|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}
. This gives

\displaystyle|\tilde{W}\tilde{H}|_{H^{s}}\lesssim|\tilde{W}|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}|\tilde{W}|_{H^{2}}^{1/2}|\tilde{H}|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|\tilde{H}|_{H^{s}}.

It is straightforward to check that
|\tilde{W}|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma+\frac{3}{2}},|\tilde{W}|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma+\frac{1}{2}},|\tilde{W}|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}
.
Note the supports of
\tilde{H}
and
\tilde{F}
belong
to
|v|\leq 8/3
. By Lemma
2.1
, we finally have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Y}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(h,f)|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}).

Combining the above estimates completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following Lemma deals with the quadratic term
in
the case when
\gamma=0
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let

\mathcal{Z}^{s}(f):=\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|f(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-f(u^{+})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u.

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{s}(f)\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}(D)f|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}).

Proof.

By the change of variable
(u,\sigma)\rightarrow(r,\tau,\varsigma)
with
u=r\tau
and
\varsigma=\frac{\sigma+\tau}{|\sigma+\tau|}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{s}(f)=4\int b^{s}(2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1)|f(r\varsigma)-f((\tau\cdot\varsigma)r\varsigma)|^{2}(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma.

Let
v=r\varsigma
, and
\theta
be the angle between
\tau
and
\varsigma
. Since
b^{s}(2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1)=b^{s}(\cos 2\theta)\lesssim(1-s)\theta^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{0\leq\theta\leq\pi/4}
, and
r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma=\sin\theta\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\mathbb{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{s}(f)\lesssim(1-s)\int_{\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}}\int_{0}^{\pi/4}\theta^{-1-2s}|f(v)-f(v\cos\theta)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\theta\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}(D)f|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}).

Here, the last inequality follows from the same argument given in
step 1 in the proof of Lemma
3.1
. More precisely,
we can consider
f(v)(f(v)-f(v\cos\theta))
and
f(v\cos\theta)(f(v)-f(v\cos\theta))

separately and apply the localization techniques used in Lemma
3.1
.
Thus, we omit the details.
∎

3.1.2.
Spherical part

We now derive some estimates related to the spherical part.
In the following Lemma, we recall a preliminary result on the characterization of norm
|(1-\Delta_{\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2}})^{s/2}f|_{L^{2}(\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2})}
. The proof of this lemma can be found in Lemma 5.5 of
[
22
]
. Note that we add an factor
s(1-s)
for
consideration of the limit
s\to 1^{-}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
f
be a smooth function defined on
\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2}
. If
0<s<1
, then it holds that

\displaystyle|f|_{L^{2}(\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2})}^{2}+s(1-s)\iint\frac{|f(\sigma)-f(\tau)|^{2}}{|\sigma-\tau|^{2+2s}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\tau\sim|(1-\Delta_{\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2}})^{s/2}f|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathop{\mathbb{S}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{2})}.

(3.6)

The constant associated to
\sim
is independent of
s
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{s}(f):=s\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|f(u)-f(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u
where
u^{+}=\frac{u+|u|\sigma}{2}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{s}(f)+|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sim|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(3.7)

Proof.

Let
r=|u|,\tau=u/|u|
and
\varsigma=\frac{\tau+\sigma}{|\tau+\sigma|}
, then
\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma=2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1
and
|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|}=r\varsigma
. For the change of variable
(u,\sigma)\rightarrow(r,\tau,\varsigma)
, one has

\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\sigma=4(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma.
Let
\theta
be the angle between
\tau
and
\sigma
, then
|\tau-\sigma|=2\sin\frac{\theta}{2},|\tau-\varsigma|=2\sin\frac{\theta}{4}
and thus

\sin\frac{\theta}{2}=\frac{1}{2}|\tau-\sigma|\leq|\tau-\varsigma|\leq|\tau-\sigma|=2\sin\frac{\theta}{2}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{|\tau-\varsigma|\leq\sqrt{2}/2}\leq(\sin\frac{\theta}{2})^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq\sqrt{2}/2}\leq 2^{2+2s}|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{|\tau-\varsigma|\leq\sqrt{2}}.

By (
3.6
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{s}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4s\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{s}(\theta)|f(r\tau)-f(r\varsigma)|^{2}(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle s(1-s)\int|f(r\tau)-f(r\varsigma)|^{2}|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{|\tau-\varsigma|\leq\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma\lesssim|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

In the last inequality, we have used the fact that
the Fourier transform and the operator
W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})
are commutable and the Plancherel’s Theorem. This gives the direction of
\lesssim
in (
3.7
).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{s}(f)\gtrsim s(1-s)\int|f(r\tau)-f(r\varsigma)|^{2}|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{|\tau-\varsigma|\leq\sqrt{2}/2}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma.

It is straightforward to check that

\displaystyle\int|f(r\tau)-f(r\varsigma)|^{2}|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}\mathrm{1}_{|\tau-\varsigma|\geq\sqrt{2}/2}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma\lesssim|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

From this and (
3.6
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{s}(f)\gtrsim s(1-s)\int|f(r\tau)-f(r\varsigma)|^{2}|\tau-\varsigma|^{-2-2s}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma-C|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This gives the direction of
\gtrsim
in (
3.7
). Hence, this completes
the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.1.3.
Upper bound of
\langle Q^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h),f\rangle

We now turn to prove the following upper bound of
Q^{s,\gamma,\eta}
.

Proposition 3.1
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle Q^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h),f\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+2s}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

Proof.

By geometric decomposition in the phase space, we have

\langle Q^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h),f\rangle=\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2}
,
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}:=\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|u|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(u)g_{*}(T_{v_{*}}h)(u)((T_{v_{*}}f)(u^{+})-(T_{v_{*}}f)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|}))\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}u,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}:=\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|u|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(u)g_{*}(T_{v_{*}}h)(u)((T_{v_{*}}f)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-(T_{v_{*}}f)(u))\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}u.

Observe that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
contain the radial and spherical parts respectively.

Step 1: Estimate of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.

By Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}\int|g_{*}|(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times(|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}f|_{L^{2}})\mathrm{d}v_{*}.

Since
\langle u+v\rangle^{s}\leq\langle u\rangle^{s}\langle v\rangle^{s}
, then for
u\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|W_{s}T_{u}f|_{L^{2}}\lesssim W_{s}(u)|W_{s}f|_{L^{2}}.

(3.8)

For
u\in\mathbb{R}^{3},l\in\mathbb{R}
,
(T_{u}W^{l})(v)=\langle v+u\rangle^{l}\lesssim C(l)\langle u\rangle^{|l|}\langle v\rangle^{l}.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle|T_{u}f|_{L^{2}_{l}}\lesssim\langle u\rangle^{|l|}|f|_{L^{2}_{l}}.

(3.9)

By (
3.8
) and (
3.9
), we have

\displaystyle|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}\lesssim W_{|\gamma|/2+s}(v_{*})|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}.

(3.10)

Since
W_{s}\in S^{s}_{1,0},W_{\gamma/2}\in S^{\gamma/2}_{1,0}
, by Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|W_{\gamma/2}W_{s}(D)T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}+|T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2-1}}

(3.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}W_{s}(D)h|_{L^{2}}+|T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle W_{|\gamma|/2}(v_{*})(|W_{\gamma/2}W_{s}(D)h|_{L^{2}}+|h|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2-1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle W_{|\gamma|/2}(v_{*})|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}},

where we have used the fact that
T_{v_{*}}
and
W_{s}(D)
are commutable, inequality (
3.9
) and Lemma
2.1
.
By (
3.10
) and (
3.11
),
we get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+2s}}(|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}})(|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}).

Step 2: Estimate of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
.

The spherical part
\mathcal{D}_{2}
has some symmetry and essentially is an 2nd-order term.
Let
u=r\tau
and
\varsigma=\frac{\tau+\sigma}{|\tau+\sigma|}
, then
\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma=2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1
and
|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|}=r\varsigma
. By the change of variable
(u,\sigma)\rightarrow(r,\tau,\varsigma)
, one has

\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}u=4(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma.

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\int r^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(r)b^{s}(2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1)(T_{v_{*}}h)(r\tau)\big((T_{v_{*}}f)(r\varsigma)-(T_{v_{*}}f)(r\tau)\big)(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int r^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(r)b^{s}(2(\tau\cdot\varsigma)^{2}-1)\big((T_{v_{*}}h)(r\tau)-(T_{v_{*}}h)(r\varsigma)\big)

\displaystyle\times\big((T_{v_{*}}f)(r\varsigma)-(T_{v_{*}}f)(r\tau)\big)(\tau\cdot\varsigma)r^{2}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\varsigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)|u|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(u)g_{*}((T_{v_{*}}h)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-(T_{v_{*}}h)(u))

\displaystyle\times((T_{v_{*}}f)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-(T_{v_{*}}f)(u))\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}u.

Then by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the fact
|u|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(u)\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}\langle u\rangle^{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta^{\gamma}\big(\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)\langle u\rangle^{\gamma}|g_{*}|((T_{v_{*}}h)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-(T_{v_{*}}h)(u))^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}u\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\big(\int b^{s}(\frac{u}{|u|}\cdot\sigma)\langle u\rangle^{\gamma}|g_{*}|((T_{v_{*}}f)(|u|\frac{u^{+}}{|u^{+}|})-(T_{v_{*}})f(u))^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}u\big)^{1/2}:=\eta^{\gamma}(\mathcal{D}_{2,1})^{1/2}(\mathcal{D}_{2,2})^{1/2}.

Note that
\mathcal{D}_{2,1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2,2}
have exactly the same structure. Hence it suffices to estimate
\mathcal{D}_{2,1}
. For this, by Lemma
3.4
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2,1}=s^{-1}\int|g_{*}|\mathcal{A}^{s}(W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h)\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim s^{-1}|g|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+2s}}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

Here we have used

\displaystyle|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})W_{\gamma/2}T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|W_{\gamma/2}W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})T_{v_{*}}h|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle W_{|\gamma|/2+s}(v_{*})(|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})W_{\gamma/2}h|_{L^{2}}+|h|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}),

because
W_{s}\in S^{s}_{1,0},W_{\gamma/2}\in S^{\gamma/2}_{1,0}
are radial functions.

Thus,

|\mathcal{D}_{2}|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}(\mathcal{D}_{2,1})^{1/2}(\mathcal{D}_{2,2})^{1/2}\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+2s}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

Combining the estimates on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
completes the
proof of the proposition.
∎

3.2.
Upper bound the operator
I^{s,\gamma,\eta}

In this subsection, we study the upper bound of
\langle I^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle
where
I^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta)
is defined by
(
2.21
) with
B^{s,\gamma,\eta}
.
For this, we first derive a slightly revised version of the
cancellation lemma introduced in
[
1
]
for the kernel

B^{s,\gamma,\eta}
.

Lemma 3.5

(Revised cancellation lemma for relative velocity away from origin)
.

Let
0<s<1,\gamma\leq 0
. For
|a|\leq-\gamma
and
0<\eta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)g_{*}(h^{\prime}-h)\mathrm{d}V|\leq C_{\gamma}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{1}_{a}}|h|_{L^{1}_{-a}}.

(3.12)

The constant
C_{\gamma}
depends on
\gamma
but is uniformly bounded for
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
.

Proof.

By the cancellation lemma in
[
1
]
, recalling
B^{s,\gamma,\eta}(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b^{s}(\theta)\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)
where
\psi^{\eta}=1-\psi_{\eta}
,
we have

\displaystyle\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)g_{*}(h^{\prime}-h)\mathrm{d}V=\int S^{s,\gamma,\eta}(v-v_{*})g_{*}h\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}v,

(3.13)

where

\displaystyle S^{s,\gamma,\eta}(v-v_{*})=\int|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b^{s}(\theta)\left(\cos^{-\gamma-3}\frac{\theta}{2}\psi^{\eta}(\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{\cos\frac{\theta}{2}})-\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\right)\mathrm{d}\sigma.

(3.14)

By using (
1.17
), we get

\displaystyle|S^{s,\gamma,\eta}(v-v_{*})|\lesssim\mathrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|/\eta\geq 3/(4\sqrt{2})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|/\eta\geq 3/(4\sqrt{2})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\lesssim_{\gamma}\mathrm{1}_{1\geq|v-v_{*}|\geq\eta\times 3/(4\sqrt{2})}\eta^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{a}\langle v\rangle^{-a}+\mathrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 1}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{a}\langle v\rangle^{-a},

which yields (
3.12
) and this completes the proof of
the lemma.
∎

Recalling the decomposition (
2.45
),
by Lemma
3.5
and Proposition
3.1
, we have
the following upper bound estimate on
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
.

Proposition 3.2
.

It holds uniformly for
1/4\leq a\leq 1/2
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{a},f)\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

The following lemma is about an integral over the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Recall
b^{s}(\theta)=(1-s)\sin^{-2-2s}\frac{\theta}{2}\mathrm{1}_{0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2}
for
0<s<1
. Denote

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi):=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{s}(\theta)\min\{|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2),1\}\mathrm{d}\sigma.

Then

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)=\mathrm{1}_{|\xi|\leq\sqrt{2}}4\pi\times 2^{s-1}|\xi|^{2}+\mathrm{1}_{|\xi|>\sqrt{2}}4\pi\times(|\xi|^{2s}+\frac{1-s}{s}(|\xi|^{2s}-2^{s})).

Hence,

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)+1\gtrsim\langle\xi\rangle^{2s},\quad A_{s}(\xi)\lesssim\mathrm{1}_{|\xi|\leq\sqrt{2}}|\xi|^{2}+\mathrm{1}_{|\xi|>\sqrt{2}}\max\{\frac{1-s}{s},1\}|\xi|^{2s}\lesssim s^{-1}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}.

Proof.

Using
\mathrm{d}\sigma=4\sin(\theta/2)\mathrm{d}\sin(\theta/2)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{S}
,
we have

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)=8\pi(1-s)\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\sin^{-1-2s}(\theta/2)\min\{|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2),1\}\mathrm{d}\sin(\theta/2).

By the change of variable:
t=\sin(\theta/2)
, we get

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)=8\pi(1-s)\int_{0}^{\sqrt{2}/{2}}t^{-1-2s}\min\{|\xi|^{2}t^{2},1\}\mathrm{d}t.

When
|\xi|\leq\sqrt{2}
, it holds

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)=8\pi(1-s)|\xi|^{2}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{2}/{2}}t^{1-2s}\mathrm{d}t=8\pi(1-s)\times\frac{2^{s-1}}{2-2s}|\xi|^{2}=4\pi\times 2^{s-1}|\xi|^{2}.

When
|\xi|>\sqrt{2}
, it holds

\displaystyle A_{s}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\pi(1-s)|\xi|^{2}\int_{0}^{|\xi|^{-1}}t^{1-2s}\mathrm{d}t+8\pi(1-s)\int_{|\xi|^{-1}}^{\sqrt{2}/{2}}t^{-1-2s}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\pi(1-s)\times(\frac{|\xi|^{2s}}{2-2s}+\frac{|\xi|^{2s}-2^{s}}{2s})=4\pi\times(|\xi|^{2s}+\frac{1-s}{s}(|\xi|^{2s}-2^{s})).

Combining the estimates in two cases completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

As for Proposition 4.2 in
[
23
]
and Proposition 3.2 in
[
12
]
, by applying
Proposition
3.1
and
Lemma
3.5
,
we can derive the upper bound of
\langle I^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle
stated in the following proposition. Note that the factor
s^{-1}
comes from Lemma
3.6
and the factor
\eta^{\gamma}
comes from

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}.

(3.15)

Proposition 3.3
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle I^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{2}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|W_{s}f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}.

The constant associated to
\lesssim
may depend on
|\beta|
but not on
s,\gamma
.

3.3.
Upper bound of
\langle\Gamma^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle

Note that

\displaystyle\Gamma^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta)=Q^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g\partial_{\beta}\mu^{1/2},h)+I^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta).

(3.16)

By Propositions
3.1
and
3.3
,
we have the following estimate.

Proposition 3.4
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\Gamma^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|g|_{L^{2}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

3.4.
Upper bound of
\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,h\rangle

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1})=\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1})+\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{2}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}):=-\Gamma^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\partial_{\beta_{1}}\mu^{1/2},f;\beta_{0}),\quad\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}):=-\Gamma^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\partial_{\beta_{1}}\mu^{1/2};\beta_{0}).

If
|\beta_{0}|=|\beta_{1}|=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f=\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f;0,0),\quad\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}f=\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}(f;0,0),\quad\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{2}f=\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{2}(f;0,0).

By using Proposition
3.4
, we have the following estimates.

Proposition 3.5
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}),h\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

Proposition 3.6
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{2}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}),h\rangle|\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|\mu^{1/32}f|_{L^{2}}|\mu^{1/32}h|_{L^{2}}.

(3.17)

Proof.

Since the proof is similar to the one of Proposition
2.6
, we omit the details. Here, we do not have the factor
s^{-1}
because of the cancellation Lemma
3.5
.
∎

Propositions
3.5
and
3.6
give the following estimate.

Proposition 3.7
.

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}),h\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\gamma}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

3.5.
Upper bound of
Q^{s,\gamma},I^{s,\gamma},\Gamma^{s,\gamma},\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma}

By applying Propositions
2.1
and
3.1
,
by taking
\eta=1
and
C_{\delta,s,\gamma}=\delta^{-1/2}s^{-1}(\gamma+2s+3)^{-1}
,
we have the following upper bound estimate on
Q^{s,\gamma}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}\in\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits
satisfying
l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3}=0
. Let
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, for any combination
a_{1},a_{2}\geq s
satisfying the constraint

a_{1}+a_{2}=s+\frac{3}{2}+\delta
,
it holds that

\displaystyle|\langle Q^{s,\gamma}(g,h),f\rangle|\lesssim C_{\delta,s,\gamma}|g|_{H^{a_{1}}_{l_{1}}}|h|_{H^{a_{2}}_{l_{2}}}|f|_{H^{s}_{l_{3}}}+s^{-1}|g|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+2s}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

By applying Propositions
2.3
and
3.3
and
taking
\eta=1
,
we have the following upper bound estimate on
I^{s,\gamma}
.

Theorem 3.2
.

Let
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, let
(a_{1},a_{2})=(\frac{3}{2}+\delta,s)
or
(0,\frac{3}{2}+\delta)
. Then

\displaystyle|\langle I^{s,\gamma}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle|\lesssim C_{\delta,s,\gamma}|g|_{H^{a_{1}}_{-l}}|h|_{H^{a_{2}}_{-l}}|f|_{H^{s}_{-l}}+s^{-1}|g|_{L^{2}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

Also Propositions
2.4
and
3.4
with
\eta=1
give
the following upper bound estimate on
\Gamma^{s,\gamma}
.

Theorem 3.3
.

For
0<\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, let
(a_{1},a_{2})=(\frac{3}{2}+\delta,s)
or
(s,\frac{3}{2}+\delta)
. Then

\displaystyle|\langle\Gamma^{s,\gamma}(g,h;\beta),f\rangle|\lesssim_{l}C_{\delta,s,\gamma}|g|_{H^{a_{1}}_{-l}}|h|_{H^{a_{2}}_{-l}}|f|_{H^{s}_{-l}}+s^{-1}|g|_{L^{2}}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

(3.18)

And Prop.
2.7
and
3.7
with
\eta=1
give the following upper bound estimate on
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma}
.

Proposition 3.8
.

Set

\displaystyle C_{s,\gamma}=s^{-1}(\gamma+2s+3)^{-1}.

(3.19)

It holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma}(f;\beta_{0},\beta_{1}),h\rangle|\lesssim C_{s,\gamma}|h|_{s,\gamma/2}|f|_{s,\gamma/2}.

The following result will be used in Section
6
to obtain dissipation
estimate on the macroscopic component.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
P
be a polynomial function.
For any combination
a_{1},a_{2}\geq 0
satisfying the constraint

a_{1}+a_{2}=s
,
it holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\Gamma^{s,\gamma}(g,h),\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}P\rangle|\lesssim C_{s,\gamma}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}g|_{H^{a_{1}}}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}h|_{H^{a_{2}}}+s^{-1}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}g|_{L^{2}}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}h|_{L^{2}}.

(3.20)

Proof.

First note that

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma^{s,\gamma}(g,h),\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}P\rangle=\langle Q^{s,\gamma}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h),P\rangle=\langle Q^{s,\gamma}_{1}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h),P\rangle+\langle Q^{s,\gamma,1}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h),P\rangle.

Applying Proposition
2.1
with
a_{3}=s+\frac{3}{2}+\delta
and taking
l_{3}
small enough relative to the degree of
P
, we get

\displaystyle|\langle Q^{s,\gamma}_{1}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h),P\rangle|\lesssim C_{s,\gamma}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}g|_{H^{a_{1}}}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}h|_{H^{a_{2}}}.

By using (
2.26
) to
P^{\prime}-P
and
(
2.28
), thanks to the factor
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}
,
we have

\displaystyle|\langle Q^{s,\gamma,1}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h),P\rangle|\lesssim s^{-1}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}g|_{L^{2}}|\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}h|_{L^{2}}.

And this completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.
Coercivity estimate

In this section, we will prove coercivity estimate of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
for some
\eta>0
. This is a linear counterpart of the famous H-theorem near Maxwellians.
Unless otherwise specified, the parameter range is
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0,0<s<1
. The parameter
\gamma
actually can tend to
-\infty
because we consider in the domain
|v-v_{*}|\gtrsim\eta>0
.

The proof contains two parts. One is a rough coercivity estimate capturing the norm
|\cdot|_{s,\gamma/2}
with a lower order correction norm
|\cdot|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}
. The other is a spectrum-gap type estimate to recover the lower order norm
|\cdot|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}
. Accordingly, we divide this section into two subsections.

4.1.
Rough coercivity estimate

In this subsection, we will prove the rough coercivity estimate of
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
for small
\eta>0
in Theorem
4.1
.
The strategy relies on the following relation (see (
) in the proof of Theorem
4.1
):

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle+\eta^{\gamma}|f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2}),

(4.1)

where the functional
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,h):=\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}g^{2}_{*}(h^{\prime}-h)^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}.

(4.2)

If
\eta=0
, then
\psi^{\eta}=1
and we write

\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma}=\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,0}
. If
\gamma=\eta=0
, we write

\mathcal{N}^{s}=\mathcal{N}^{s,0,0}
for simplicity.
Thanks to (
4.1
),
to obtain the coercivity estimate of
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
, it suffices to estimate from below the two functionals

\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)
and
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})
.

4.1.1.
Gain of weight from
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})

The functional
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})
produces weight
W_{s}
in the phase space.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
0\leq\eta\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})+|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\geq C|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}},

where
C>0
is a generic constant.

Proof.

Let
0<\delta\leq 1
.
We consider the set
A(\delta):=\{(v_{*},v,\sigma):|v|\leq 2,|v_{*}|\geq 4,\sin(\theta/2)\leq\delta|v_{*}|^{-1}\}
. Since
|v-v_{*}|\geq 2\geq 4/3
in the set
A(\delta)
,
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})\geq\int B^{s,\gamma}\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}f_{*}^{2}((\mu^{1/2})^{\prime}-\mu^{1/2})^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

(4.3)

Note that
\nabla\mu^{1/2}=-\frac{\mu^{1/2}}{2}v
and
\nabla^{2}\mu^{1/2}=\frac{\mu^{1/2}}{4}(-2I+v\otimes v)
. By Taylor expansion (
2.26
),
using the basic inequality
(A-B)^{2}\geq A^{2}/2-B^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle(\mu^{1/2}(v^{\prime})-\mu^{1/2}(v))^{2}\geq\frac{\mu(v)}{8}|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}|(\nabla^{2}\mu^{1/2})(v(\kappa))|^{2}|v^{\prime}-v|^{4}\mathrm{d}\kappa.

Plugging this into (
4.3
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\int B^{s,\gamma}\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}\mu(v)|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|^{2}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}V

(4.4)

\displaystyle-\int B^{s,\gamma}\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}|(\nabla^{2}\mu^{1/2})(v(\kappa))|^{2}|v^{\prime}-v|^{4}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}V\mathrm{d}\kappa

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)-\mathcal{I}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(\delta).

To estimate
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)
, for fixed
v,v_{*}
, we introduce an orthogonal basis
(h^{1}_{v,v_{*}},h^{2}_{v,v_{*}},\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|})
such that
\mathrm{d}\sigma=\sin\theta\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\phi
. Then one has

\displaystyle\frac{v^{\prime}-v}{|v^{\prime}-v|}=\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\phi h^{1}_{v,v_{*}}+\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\phi h^{2}_{v,v_{*}}-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|},

and

\displaystyle\frac{v}{|v|}=c_{1}h^{1}_{v,v_{*}}+c_{2}h^{2}_{v,v_{*}}+c_{3}\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|},

where
c_{3}=\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}
and
c_{1},c_{2}
are constants independent of
\theta
and
\phi
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v^{\prime}-v}{|v^{\prime}-v|}=c_{1}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\phi+c_{2}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\phi-c_{3}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle|\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v^{\prime}-v}{|v^{\prime}-v|}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{2}_{1}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\cos^{2}\phi+c^{2}_{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\phi+c^{2}_{3}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle+2c_{1}c_{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\cos\phi\sin\phi-2c_{3}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}(c_{1}\cos\phi+c_{2}\sin\phi).

Since
|v^{\prime}-v|=|v-v_{*}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2}
and
\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, by integrating with respect to
\sigma
and using
b^{s}(\theta)=(1-s)\sin^{-2-2s}\frac{\theta}{2}\mathrm{1}_{0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}b^{s}(\theta)\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\theta\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\phi\mathrm{d}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\pi(c^{2}_{1}+c^{2}_{2})|v|^{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\int_{0}^{\pi}b^{s}(\theta)\sin^{3}\frac{\theta}{2}\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}\mathrm{d}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\delta^{2-2s}(c^{2}_{1}+c^{2}_{2})|v_{*}|^{2s-2}|v|^{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\mathrm{1}_{B(\delta)},

where
B(\delta)=\{(v_{*},v):|v_{*}|\geq 4,|v|\leq 2\}
.
Note that

\displaystyle(1-c_{3}^{2})|v-v_{*}|^{2}=(1-(\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v_{*}}{|v_{*}|})^{2})|v_{*}|^{2}

gives

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\gtrsim\delta^{2-2s}(1-(\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v_{*}}{|v_{*}|})^{2})|v_{*}|^{2s}|v|^{2}\mathrm{1}_{B(\delta)}.

Plugging this estimate in the definition of

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)\gtrsim\int\delta^{2-2s}(1-(\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v_{*}}{|v_{*}|})^{2})|v_{*}|^{2s}|v|^{2}\mathrm{1}_{B(\delta)}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu(v)f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}.

Note that in the region
B(\delta)
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}|v_{*}|\leq|v-v_{*}|\leq\frac{3}{2}|v_{*}|.

(4.5)

We then obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\delta^{2-2s}\int(1-(\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v_{*}}{|v_{*}|})^{2})|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}|v|^{2}\mathrm{1}_{B(\delta)}\mu(v)f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\delta^{2-2s}\int|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*},

where we have used the fact that
\int(1-(\frac{v}{|v|}\cdot\frac{v_{*}}{|v_{*}|})^{2})|v|^{2}\mu(v)\mathrm{1}_{|v|\leq 2}\mathrm{d}v>0
which is independent of
v_{*}
.

We now turn to estimate
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)
.
By (
4.5
) and
|v(\kappa)-v_{*}|\leq|v-v_{*}|
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}\leq\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}\mathrm{1}_{\sin(\theta/2)\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta|v-v_{*}|^{-1}}.

Recalling
B^{s,\gamma}=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b^{s}(\theta),|v^{\prime}-v|=|v-v_{*}|\sin(\theta/2)
, by (
4.5
), and by using
|\nabla^{2}\mu^{1/2}|\lesssim\mu^{1/4}
and
the change of variable
v\to v(\kappa)
in Lemma
2.2
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b^{s}(\theta)\mathrm{1}_{A(\delta)}|(\nabla^{2}\mu^{1/2})(v(\kappa))|^{2}|v^{\prime}-v|^{4}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}V\mathrm{d}\kappa

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\sin^{4}(\theta/2)\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}\mathrm{1}_{\sin(\theta/2)\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta|v-v_{*}|^{-1}}|v-v_{*}|^{4-2s}\mu^{1/2}(v(\kappa))|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}V\mathrm{d}\kappa

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\sin^{4}(\theta/2)\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}\mathrm{1}_{\sin(\theta/2)\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta|v-v_{*}|^{-1}\psi^{-1}_{\kappa}(\theta)}|v-v_{*}|^{4-2s}\psi^{7-2s}_{\kappa}(\theta)\mu^{1/2}(v)|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\ \mathrm{d}V\mathrm{d}\kappa

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta^{4-2s}\int\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}\mu^{1/2}(v)|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim\delta^{4-2s}\left(\int\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\right).

Combining the estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{s,\gamma}(\delta)
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})\geq\left(C_{1}-C_{2}\delta^{2}\right)\delta^{2-2s}\int\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*},

for some generic constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. By choosing
\delta
such that
C_{2}\delta^{2}=C_{1}/2
, and observing

|v_{*}|^{\gamma+2s}\sim\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma+2s}
for
|v_{*}|\geq 4
,
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})\gtrsim\int\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|\geq 4}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}=(|f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}-\int\mathrm{1}_{|v_{*}|<4}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma+2s}f_{*}^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{*}).

(4.6)

If
|v_{*}|\leq 4
, then
\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma+2s}\sim\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}
. Then the proof of the proposition is completed.
∎

In the following, we focus on gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},f)
. The strategy can be stated as follows.

(1)

Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
;

(2)

Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)
by reducing to
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
;

(3)

Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,f)
by reducing to
\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)
.

4.1.2.
Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
.

We derive Sobolev regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
by the following argument used in
[
1
]
. For
g\geq 0
with
|g|_{L^{1}}\geq\delta>0
and
|g|_{L^{1}_{1}}\leq\lambda<\infty
, there exists a constant
C(\delta,\lambda)>0
such that

\displaystyle\int b(\cos\theta)g_{*}(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\mathrm{d}v+|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq C(\delta,\lambda)|a(D)f|_{L^{2}}^{2},

(4.7)

where
a(\xi):=\int b(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)\min\{|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2),1\}\mathrm{d}\sigma+1
. By applying (
4.7
) to the angular function
b^{s}

and using
Lemma
3.6
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
g
be a function such that
|g|_{L^{2}}\geq\delta>0,|g|_{L^{2}_{1/2}}\leq\lambda<\infty
, then there is a constant
C(\delta,\lambda)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)+|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq C(\delta,\lambda)|f|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

(4.8)

We now extract
the anisotropic norm
|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}
from
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
by Bobylev’s formula and the upper bound of the radial part.

Lemma 4.2
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)+|g|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}(|W_{s}(D)f|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}f|^{2}_{L^{2}})\gtrsim|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.9)

Proof.

By Bobylev’s formula, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int b^{s}(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)(\widehat{g^{2}}(0)|\hat{f}(\xi)-\hat{f}(\xi^{+})|^{2}+2\Re((\widehat{g^{2}}(0)-\widehat{g^{2}}(\xi^{-}))\hat{f}(\xi^{+})\bar{\hat{f}}(\xi))\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\mathcal{I}_{1}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{3}}\mathcal{I}_{2},

where
\xi^{+}=\frac{\xi+|\xi|\sigma}{2}
and
\xi^{-}=\frac{\xi-|\xi|\sigma}{2}
.
Note that
\widehat{g^{2}}(0)-\widehat{g^{2}}(\xi^{-})=\int(1-\cos(v\cdot\xi^{-}))g^{2}(v)\mathrm{d}v
and

1-\cos(v\cdot\xi^{-})\lesssim\min\{|v|^{2}|\xi|^{2}|\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}-\sigma|^{2},1\}\sim\min\{|v|^{2}|\xi^{+}|^{2}|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|}-\sigma|^{2},1\}.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
the change of variable
\xi\rightarrow\xi^{+}
, using
Lemma
3.6
and the fact that
W_{s}(|v||\xi|)\lesssim W_{s}(|v|)W_{s}(|\xi|)
,
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{2}|\lesssim s^{-1}\int(W_{s})^{2}(|v||\xi|)|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}g^{2}(v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim s^{-1}|W_{s}g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|W_{s}(D)f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.10)

Now we turn to estimate
\mathcal{I}_{1}
. By the geometric decomposition

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\xi)-\hat{f}(\xi^{+})=\hat{f}(\xi)-\hat{f}(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})+\hat{f}(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})-\hat{f}(\xi^{+}),

(4.11)

and using
(A+B)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}A^{2}-B^{2}
,
we have
\mathcal{I}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}-\mathcal{I}_{1,2}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}:=\int b^{s}(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)|\hat{f}(\xi)-\hat{f}(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\xi,\quad\mathcal{I}_{1,2}:=\int b^{s}(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma)|\hat{f}(|\xi|\frac{\xi^{+}}{|\xi^{+}|})-\hat{f}(\xi^{+})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}\xi.

By Lemma
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}+s^{-1}|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sim s^{-1}|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.12)

By Lemma
3.2
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2}\lesssim s^{-1}(|W_{s}(D)\hat{f}|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}\hat{f}|^{2}_{L^{2}})=s^{-1}(|W_{s}(D)f|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}).

(4.13)

Combining (
4.10
), (
4.13
) and (
4.12
) gives (
4.9
).
∎

4.1.3.
Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)

We first introduce some notations. Recall
\psi_{R}(v):=\psi(v/R)
.
Let
\psi_{r,u}(v):=\psi_{r}(v-u)
and
\phi_{R,r,u}:=\psi_{14R}-\psi_{4r,u}
for some
r,R>0
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. The following lemma gives some
bound estimates on
\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)
by
\mathcal{N}^{s}(g,f)
from below provided the distance between supports of
g
and
f
is suitably large.

Lemma 4.3
.

For
0\leq\eta\leq 1\leq R
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)+|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}g,(1-\psi_{4R})f).

(4.14)

For
0\leq\eta\leq r\leq 1\leq R,u\in B_{6R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)+r^{-2}R^{2}|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}g,\psi_{r,u}f).

(4.15)

Proof.

We proceed in the spirit of
[
21
]
. Note that

\psi_{R}
is supported in
|v|\leq\frac{4}{3}R
and equals to
1
in
|v|\leq\frac{3}{4}R
.

1-\psi_{R}
is supported in
|v|\geq\frac{3}{4}R
and equals to
1
in
|v|\geq\frac{4}{3}R
.
If
|v_{*}|\leq\frac{4}{3}R
and
|v|\geq 3R
, then
|v-v_{*}|\geq\frac{5}{3}R\geq\frac{4}{3}\eta
, which gives
\psi_{R}(v_{*})(1-\psi_{4R}(v))\leq\textrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 4\eta/3}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\textrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 4\eta/3}g^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)(\psi_{R}g)^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}(1-\psi_{4R})^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int b^{s}(\theta)(\psi_{R}g)^{2}_{*}(((1-\psi_{4R})f)^{\prime}-(1-\psi_{4R})f)^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle-\int b^{s}(\theta)(\psi_{R}g)^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime})^{2}(\psi_{4R}^{\prime}-\psi_{4R})^{2}\mathrm{d}V:=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{I}_{1}-\mathcal{I}_{2}.

Observe that
\mathcal{I}_{1}=\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}g,(1-\psi_{4R})f)
. Since
|\nabla\psi_{4R}|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim R^{-1}|\nabla\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim R^{-1}
, we get
(\psi_{R})^{2}_{*}(\psi_{4R}^{\prime}-\psi_{4R})^{2}\lesssim R^{-2}|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}=R^{-2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)
. If
|v_{*}|\leq\frac{4}{3}R\leq 2R,|v|\geq 20R,0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
, we have

\displaystyle|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|=\cos(\theta/2)|v-v_{*}|\geq\cos(\theta/2)(|v|-|v_{*}|)\geq 9\sqrt{2}R.

Then we have

|v^{\prime}|\geq|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|-|v_{*}|\geq 9\sqrt{2}R-2R\geq 6R,

which gives
\psi_{4R}(v^{\prime})=0=\psi_{4R}(v)
. Since
\theta\leq\pi/2
, we have

\displaystyle(\psi_{R})^{2}_{*}(\psi_{4R}^{\prime}-\psi_{4R})^{2}\leq\mathrm{1}_{|v|\leq 20R,|v_{*}|\leq 2R}R^{-2}|\nabla\psi|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)\lesssim\sin^{2}(\theta/2).

By the change of variable
v\to v^{\prime}
and using (
1.17
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}\lesssim\int g^{2}_{*}f^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

This together with the fact that
\mathcal{I}_{1}=\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}g,(1-\psi_{4R})f)
give (
4.14
).

If
v\in\mathrm{supp}\psi_{r,u},v_{*}\in\mathrm{supp}\phi_{R,r,u}
, we claim
|v-v_{*}|\geq r\geq\eta
.
In fact, if
v\in\mathrm{supp}\psi_{r,u}
, then
|v-u|\leq\frac{4}{3}r
. If
|v_{*}-u|\leq 3r=\frac{3}{4}\times 4r
, then

\psi_{4r,u}(v_{*})=1
. Moreover,

|v_{*}|\leq|u|+|u-v_{*}|\leq 6R+3r\leq 9R\leq\frac{3}{4}\times 14R
, then
\psi_{14R}(v_{*})=1
. As a result,
\phi_{R,r,u}(v_{*})=0
. From this if

v_{*}\in\mathrm{supp}\phi_{R,r,u}
, then

|v_{*}-u|\geq 3r
. Therefore,
if
v\in\mathrm{supp}\psi_{r,u},v_{*}\in\mathrm{supp}\phi_{R,r,u}
, then
|v-v_{*}|\geq|v_{*}-u|-|v-u|\geq\frac{5}{3}r\geq\frac{4}{3}\eta
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(g,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\textrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq 4\eta/3}g^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)(\phi_{R,r,u}g)^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}\psi^{2}_{r,u}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int b^{s}(\theta)(\phi_{R,r,u}g)^{2}_{*}((\psi_{r,u}f)^{\prime}-\psi_{r,u}f)^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle-\int b^{s}(\theta)(\phi_{R,r,u}g)^{2}_{*}(f^{\prime})^{2}(\psi_{r,u}^{\prime}-\psi_{r,u})^{2}\mathrm{d}V:=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{J}_{1}-\mathcal{J}_{2}.

Observe that
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}g,\psi_{r,u}f)
.
Since
|\nabla\psi_{r,u}(v)|\lesssim r^{-1}|\nabla\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\mathrm{1}_{3r/4\leq|v-u|\leq 4r/3}
, by Taylor expansion, we get

\displaystyle|\psi_{r,u}^{\prime}-\psi_{r,u}|^{2}=|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\psi_{r,u}\left(v(\kappa)\right)\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)\mathrm{d}\kappa|^{2}\lesssim r^{-2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{1}_{3r/4\leq|v(\kappa)-u|\leq 4r/3}\mathrm{d}\kappa.

For
u\in B_{6R},|v_{*}|\leq 20R,3r/4\leq|v(\kappa)-u|\leq 4r/3
, we have

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|\leq\sqrt{2}|v(\kappa)-v_{*}|\leq\sqrt{2}|v(\kappa)-u|+\sqrt{2}|u-v_{*}|\leq 4\sqrt{2}r/3+\sqrt{2}(6R+20R)\leq 28\sqrt{2}R,

and

\displaystyle(\phi_{R,r,u})^{2}_{*}(\psi_{r,u}^{\prime}-\psi_{r,u})^{2}\lesssim r^{-2}R^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2).

By the change of variable
v\rightarrow v^{\prime}
and (
1.17
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}\lesssim r^{-2}R^{2}\int g^{2}_{*}f^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim r^{-2}R^{2}|g|^{2}_{L^{2}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

This together with the fact that
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}g,\psi_{r,u}f)
give (
4.15
).
∎

4.1.4.
Gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)

We first establish a relation between
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
and
\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

For
\gamma\leq 0\leq\eta
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,f)+|g|^{2}_{L^{2}_{|\gamma/2|+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{\gamma/2}g,W_{\gamma/2}f).

Proof.

Set
F=W_{\gamma/2}f
. If
\gamma\leq 0
, then
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\geq\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,f)\geq\int b^{s}(\theta)\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}g^{2}_{*}((W_{-\gamma/2}F)^{\prime}-W_{-\gamma/2}F)^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

We apply the following decomposition

\displaystyle(W_{-\gamma/2}F)^{\prime}-W_{-\gamma/2}F=(W_{-\gamma/2})^{\prime}(F^{\prime}-F)+F(W_{-\gamma/2}^{\prime}-W_{-\gamma/2}):=A+B.

Using
(A+B)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}A^{2}-B^{2}
, we get
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,f)\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{I}_{1}-\mathcal{I}_{2}
,
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}g^{2}_{*}W_{-\gamma}^{\prime}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\mathrm{d}V,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}g^{2}_{*}F^{2}(W_{-\gamma/2}^{\prime}-W_{-\gamma/2})^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

Since
\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}\lesssim\langle v_{*}-v^{\prime}\rangle^{\gamma}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{-\gamma}\sim\langle v_{*}-v\rangle^{\gamma}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{-\gamma}
,
we get
\mathcal{I}_{1}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{\gamma/2}g,W_{\gamma/2}f)
.
Taylor expansion implies that

\displaystyle(W_{-\gamma/2}^{\prime}-W_{-\gamma/2})^{2}\lesssim|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)\int\langle v(\kappa)\rangle^{-\gamma-2}\mathrm{d}\kappa.

Note that

\displaystyle\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\langle v(\kappa)\rangle^{-\gamma-2}\lesssim\langle v(\kappa)-v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma+2}v(\kappa)\rangle^{-\gamma-2}\lesssim\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|+2}.

By the above estimate and (
1.17
),
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}\lesssim\int g^{2}_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|+2}F^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim|g|^{2}_{L^{2}_{|\gamma/2|+1}}|F|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Combining the estimates on
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}

completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We are now ready to derive gain of regularity from
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0\leq\eta\leq\eta_{1}:=\frac{9}{16}(2\pi)^{1/2}(\frac{1}{4}W_{-5}(3/4)\mu(3/4))^{1/3}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})W_{\gamma/2}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(4.16)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(g,f)+|g|^{2}_{L^{2}_{|\gamma/2|+1}}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\geq C\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{\gamma/2}g,W_{\gamma/2}f),

(4.17)

where
C
is a generic constant.
Taking
g=\mu^{1/2}
in
(
4.17
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{\gamma/2}\mu^{1/2},W_{\gamma/2}f)\geq\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},W_{\gamma/2}f).

Taking
g=W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},f=F:=W_{\gamma/2}f
in Lemma
4.3
, we have for
\eta\leq r\leq 1\leq R,u\in B_{6R}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},F)+|F|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},(1-\psi_{4R})F),

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,0,\eta}(W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},F)+r^{-2}R^{2}|F|^{2}_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2},\psi_{r,u}F).

(4.19)

From now on, take
R=1
, then
\psi_{R}=\psi
, we get

\displaystyle|\psi_{R}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\int\psi^{2}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v\geq\frac{4\pi}{3}(3/4)^{3}W_{-5}(3/4)\mu(3/4):=\delta^{2}_{*}.

Recalling
\phi_{R,r,u}=\psi_{14R}-\psi_{4r,u}
and
\psi_{14R}\geq\psi_{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\int\phi^{2}_{R,r,u}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v\geq\frac{1}{2}\int\psi^{2}_{14R}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v-\int\psi^{2}_{3r,u}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}_{*}-\int\psi^{2}_{3r,u}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v.

Note that
\int\psi^{2}_{3r,u}W_{-5}\mu\mathrm{d}v\leq\frac{4\pi}{3}(\frac{4}{3}r)^{3}(2\pi)^{-\frac{3}{2}}:=Cr^{3}
.
By choosing
r
such that
Cr^{3}=\frac{1}{4}\delta^{2}_{*}
, we get

\displaystyle|\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq\delta^{2}_{*}/4.

Note that
r=\frac{9}{16}(2\pi)^{1/2}(\frac{1}{4}W_{-5}(3/4)\mu(3/4))^{1/3}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\min\{|\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|_{L^{2}},|\psi_{R}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|_{L^{2}}\}\geq\delta_{*}/2.

(4.20)

On the other hand, note that

\displaystyle\max\{|\psi_{R}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|_{L^{2}_{1}},|\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-5/2}\mu^{1/2}|_{L^{2}_{1}}\}\leq|\mu|_{L^{1}_{2}}^{1/2}:=\lambda_{*}.

(4.21)

Thanks to (
4.20
) and (
4.21
), by Lemma
4.1
,
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}W_{-3/2}\mu^{1/2},(1-\psi_{4R})F)+|(1-\psi_{4R})F|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq C(\delta_{*}/2,\lambda_{*})|(1-\psi_{4R})F|^{2}_{H^{s}},

(4.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-3/2}\mu^{1/2},\psi_{r,u}F)+|\psi_{r,u}F|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq C(\delta_{*}/2,\lambda_{*})|\psi_{r,u}F|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

(4.23)

Note that
1-\psi_{4R}(v)=1
if
|v|\geq 6R\geq\frac{16}{3}R
.
There is a finite cover of
B_{6R}
with open ball
B_{r}(u_{j})
for
u_{j}\in B_{6R}
. More precisely,
there exists
\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}\subset B_{6R}
such that
B_{6R}\subset\cup_{j=1}^{N}B_{r}(u_{j})
, where
N\sim\frac{1}{r^{3}}
is a generic constant. We then have
\psi_{4R}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\psi_{r,u_{j}}
and thus

|\psi_{4R}F|^{2}_{H^{s}}\leq N\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\psi_{r,u_{j}}F|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

From this together with (
4.18
), (
4.19
), (
4.22
), (
4.23
),
we get for any
0\leq\eta\leq r
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\gtrsim r^{8}|W_{\gamma/2}f|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Since
r
is a generic constant, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\gtrsim|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(4.24)

Thanks to (
4.20
) and (
4.21
),
by (
4.9
) in Lemma
4.2
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(\psi_{R}W_{-3/2}\mu^{1/2},(1-\psi_{4R})F)+\lambda^{2}_{*}(|(1-\psi_{4R})F|^{2}_{H^{s}}+|(1-\psi_{4R})F|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\delta^{2}_{*}|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})(1-\psi_{4R})F|^{2}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}(\phi_{R,r,u}W_{-3/2}\mu^{1/2},\psi_{r,u}F)+\lambda^{2}_{*}(|\psi_{r,u}F|^{2}_{H^{s}}+|\psi_{r,u}F|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}})\gtrsim\delta^{2}_{*}|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})\psi_{r,u}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

By applying the similar argument, we also have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|W_{s}(D)W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|W_{s}W_{\gamma/2}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim|W_{s}((-\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{1/2})W_{\gamma/2}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(4.25)

Finally a suitable combination of (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) gives (
4.16
).
∎

4.1.5.
Rough coercivity estimate of
\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle

By Lemma
4.1
and (
4.16
) in Lemma
4.5
, we have the following estimate.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0\leq\eta\leq\eta_{1}
where
\eta_{1}
is the constant in Lemma
4.5
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\gtrsim|f|_{s,\gamma/2}^{2}.

(4.26)

Now we are ready to prove the following rough coercivity estimate.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0<\eta\leq\eta_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle+\eta^{\gamma}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\gtrsim|f|_{s,\gamma/2}^{2}.

(4.27)

Proof.

We recall that
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})
corresponds to
the anisotropic norm
|||\cdot|||
introduced in
[
2
]
.
By the proof of Proposition 2.16 in
[
2
]
,

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}f,f\rangle\geq\frac{1}{10}(\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2}))-\frac{3}{10}\big|\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}\mu_{*}(f^{2}-f^{\prime 2})\mathrm{d}V\big|.

By the cancellation Lemma
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}\mu_{*}(f^{2}-f^{\prime 2})\mathrm{d}V\big|\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{1}f,f\rangle\geq\frac{1}{10}(\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2}))-C\eta^{\gamma}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}.

(4.28)

By Proposition
3.6
, we get

\displaystyle|\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}_{2}f,f\rangle|\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|\mu^{1/8}f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\eta^{\gamma}|f|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}.

(4.29)

(
4.28
) and (
4.29
) imply (
4.1
).
Then by applying Lemma
4.6
, we complete the proof of the theorem.
∎

4.2.
Spectrum-gap type estimate

In this subsection, we consider the coercivity estimates of
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
in the microscopic space. This is also referred as the ”spectral gap” estimate.

Recall
\mathrm{ker}(\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma}_{B})=\mathrm{ker}(\mathcal{L}^{\gamma}_{L})=\mathrm{span}\{\sqrt{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}v_{1},\sqrt{\mu}v_{2},\sqrt{\mu}v_{3},\sqrt{\mu}|v|^{2}\}:=\mathrm{ker}
.
An orthonormal basis of
\mathrm{ker}
can be chosen as

\{\sqrt{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}v_{1},\sqrt{\mu}v_{2},\sqrt{\mu}v_{3},\sqrt{\mu}(|v|^{2}-3)/\sqrt{6}\}:=\{e_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq 5}.

The projection operator
\mathbf{P}
on the kernel space
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}f:=\sum_{j=1}^{5}\langle f,e_{j}\rangle e_{j}=(a+b\cdot v+c|v|^{2})\sqrt{\mu},

(4.30)

where for
1\leq i\leq 3
,

\displaystyle a=\int_{\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}}(\frac{5}{2}-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2})\sqrt{\mu}f\mathrm{d}v;\quad b_{i}=\int_{\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}}v_{i}\sqrt{\mu}f\mathrm{d}v;\quad c=\int_{\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}}(\frac{|v|^{2}}{6}-\frac{1}{2})\sqrt{\mu}f\mathrm{d}v.

(4.31)

We will show that the lower order term
|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}
in
(
4.27
) can be dropped for
f\in\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}
.

The idea is to firstly consider the case when
\gamma=0
case and then to use mathematical induction for the general
\gamma<0
case.

4.2.1.
The case
\gamma=0

This case is clear, cf. the explicit spectrum computation by Wang-Chang
[
33
]
, in which the authors showed that the smallest positive eigenvalue is bounded from below by
\int b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma
upto a multiplicative factor.
Recalling (
1.17
), it holds for
f\in\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0,0}f,f\rangle\geq\lambda_{1}|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By the proof of Theorem
4.1
for the case of
\gamma=0
, we can also take
\eta=0
to get

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0,0}f,f\rangle+|f|_{L^{2}}^{2}\gtrsim|f|^{2}_{s,0}.

Hence, there exists a generic constant
\lambda_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0,0}f,f\rangle\geq\lambda_{2}|f|^{2}_{s,0}.

(4.32)

We now show that
\mathcal{L}^{s,0,\eta}
also satisfies the above estimate if
\eta
is small enough.
For this,
we prove smallness of
\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0}_{\eta}f,f\rangle
when
\eta
is small.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
0\leq\eta\leq 1
, then it holds for
f\in\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}

that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0}_{\eta}f,f\rangle\lesssim\eta^{3}|f|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

Proof.

Firstly, note that

\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0}_{\eta}f,f\rangle\leq 2\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)+2\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})
, where
the functional
\mathcal{N}^{s,\gamma}_{\eta}
is defined in (
2.44
).
By (
2.46
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\eta}(\mu^{1/2},f)\lesssim\eta^{2s+3}|f|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

Recall

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})=\int b^{s}(\theta)\psi_{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)f^{2}_{*}((\mu^{1/2})^{\prime}-\mu^{1/2})^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

By using

|(\mu^{1/2})^{\prime}-\mu^{1/2}|\lesssim|v-v_{*}|\theta
and (
1.17
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\eta}(f,\mu^{1/2})\lesssim\int b^{s}(\theta)\psi_{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)f^{2}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\theta^{2}\mathrm{d}V\lesssim\int\mathrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\leq 4\eta/3}f^{2}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim\eta^{5}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Combining the above estimates completes the proof.
∎

From (
4.32
), by taking
\eta
small enough in Lemma
4.7
,
we get the following coercivity estimate.

Lemma 4.8
.

There is a generic constant
\eta_{2}>0
such that
for
f\in\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,0,\eta_{2}}f,f\rangle\geq\lambda_{0}|f|^{2}_{s,0}.

4.2.2.
The general case
\gamma<0

The coercivity estimate of
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
in the
\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}
space for
\gamma<0
can be stated as follows.

Theorem 4.2
.

For
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
, with
the constant
\eta_{1}
defined
in Theorem
4.1
and the constants
\eta_{2},\lambda_{0}
defined
in Lemma
4.8
,
let
\eta=\min\{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\}
.
There is a generic constant
0<c<1
such that for any
\gamma\in[-5,0]
satisfying

-ks\leq\gamma<-(k-1)s
for some integer
k\geq 0
,
it holds for
f\in\mathrm{ker}^{\perp}

that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle\geq c^{2^{k}-1}s^{2^{k}-1}\lambda_{0}^{2^{k}}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.33)

Remark 4.1
.

Theorem
4.2
indeed holds for any
\gamma\leq 0
even though we only need it for
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
.
The analysis can also be applied to the case when
\gamma>0
.

For later use, set

\lambda_{s,\gamma}:=c^{2^{k}-1}s^{2^{k}-1}\lambda_{0}^{2^{k}}
.
The following remark is about the lower bound of
\lambda_{s,\gamma}
.

Remark 4.2
.

For
-2s-3\gamma\leq-2s
, as
-\gamma/s\leq 2+3/s
, then

\displaystyle\lambda_{s,\gamma}\geq c^{2^{\lceil 2+3/s\rceil}-1}s^{2^{\lceil 2+3/s\rceil}-1}\lambda_{0}^{2^{\lceil 2+3/s\rceil}}:=\lambda_{s}.

(4.34)

Here
\lceil a\rceil
is the smallest integer no less than
a
. Note that
\lambda_{s}
is non-decreasing with respect to
s
.

Motivated by
[
2
,
23
]
about the exchanging the
kinetic component in the cross-section with a weight of velocity on the function, we can
apply an induction argument based on the estimate for the case
\gamma=0

obtained in Lemma
4.8
and the gain of moment of order
s
. As the first step, we reduce the case when
-s\leq\gamma<0
to
\gamma=0
, and then by induction to cover the whole range
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
. For this, we first introduce a scaled weight function

\displaystyle U_{\delta}(v):=W(\delta v)=(1+\delta^{2}|v|^{2})^{1/2}\geq\max\{\delta|v|,1\}.

(4.35)

Here
\delta
is a sufficiently small parameter to be chosen later. We now give two technical lemmas on some integrals involving
U_{\delta}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
-5\leq\alpha,\beta<0<s,\delta<1
.
Recall
b^{s}(\theta):=(1-s)\sin^{-2-2s}\frac{\theta}{2}\mathrm{1}_{0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2}
.
Set

\displaystyle X(\beta,\delta):=\delta^{-\beta}\left((U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})^{\prime}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})^{\prime}_{*}-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})_{*}\right)^{2}.

Then for
v\in\mathop{\mathbb{R}\kern 0.0pt}\nolimits^{3}
,

\displaystyle\int b^{s}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)X(\beta,\delta)\mu_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2s}\eta^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta+2s}.

(4.36)

Proof.

First, it is straightforward to check

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\mu_{*}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\alpha}\mu_{*}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\alpha|}\mu_{*}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}\mu_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Note that

\displaystyle X(\beta,\delta)\lesssim\delta^{-\beta}(U^{\beta}_{\delta})^{\prime}_{*}\left((U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})^{\prime}-(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\right)^{2}+\delta^{-\beta}U^{\beta}_{\delta}\left((U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})^{\prime}_{*}-(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})_{*}\right)^{2}:=A_{1}+A_{2}.

We only estimate
A_{1}
because
A_{2}
can be estimated similarly.

For
a\leq 0
, one has

\displaystyle|\nabla U^{a}_{\delta}|\lesssim|a|\delta U^{a}_{\delta},

(4.37)

which gives

\displaystyle\big((U^{a}_{\delta})^{\prime}-(U^{a}_{\delta})\big)^{2}=\big|\int_{0}^{1}(\nabla U^{a}_{\delta})(v(\kappa))\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)\mathrm{d}\kappa\big|^{2}\lesssim a^{2}\delta^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}U^{2a}_{\delta}(v(\kappa))\mathrm{d}\kappa.

Thanks to
|v^{\prime}_{*}|^{2}+|v(\kappa)|^{2}\sim|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta^{-2a}(U^{2a}_{\delta})^{\prime}_{*}U^{2a}_{\delta}(v(\kappa))\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{2a},

(4.38)

which gives

\displaystyle\delta^{-2a}(U^{2a}_{\delta})^{\prime}_{*}\big((U^{a}_{\delta})^{\prime}-(U^{a}_{\delta})\big)^{2}\lesssim a^{2}\delta^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2a}.

(4.39)

Divide the integral
\int b^{s}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}A_{1}\mu_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}
into two parts:
\mathcal{I}_{\leq}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\geq}
corresponding to
\delta|v-v_{*}|\leq 1
and
\delta|v-v_{*}|\geq 1
.
When
\delta|v-v_{*}|\leq 1
, using (
4.39
) for
a=\beta/2
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\leq}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{2}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta}\int\mathrm{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\delta^{-1}}b^{s}(\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{2s}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta+2s}.

When
\delta|v-v_{*}|\geq 1
. We further divide the integral
\mathcal{I}_{\geq}
into two parts:

\mathcal{I}_{\geq,\leq}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\geq,\geq}
corresponding to

\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq\delta^{-1}|v-v_{*}|^{-1}
and

\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\geq\delta^{-1}|v-v_{*}|^{-1}
respectively.
By using (
4.39
) for
a=\beta/2
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\geq,\leq}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{2}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta}\int\mathrm{1}_{\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq\delta^{-1}|v-v_{*}|^{-1}}b^{s}(\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{2s}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta+2s}.

For the remainder with
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\geq\delta^{-1}|v-v_{*}|^{-1}
, it holds from (
4.38
) that

A_{1}\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{\beta}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\geq,\geq}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta}\int\mathrm{1}_{\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\geq\delta^{-1}|v-v_{*}|^{-1}}b^{s}(\theta)\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim s^{-1}\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{2s}\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+\beta+2s}.

Combining the above estimates completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
-5\leq\alpha,\beta<0<s,\eta,\delta<1
.
Set
\varphi_{\beta,\delta}:=(1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:=\int b^{s}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)(\varphi_{\beta,\delta}^{\prime}-\varphi_{\beta,\delta})^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}\eta^{\alpha}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\alpha+2s}.

Proof.

By (
4.37
), we get

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\beta,\delta}|\lesssim|\beta|\delta\mu^{\frac{1}{4}},\quad|\nabla\varphi_{\beta,\delta}|\lesssim|\beta|\delta\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(4.40)

From this, we first have

\displaystyle(\varphi_{\beta,\delta}^{\prime}-\varphi_{\beta,\delta})^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}((\mu^{1/2})^{\prime}+\mu^{1/2}).

(4.41)

By 1st-order Taylor expansion,
we get

\displaystyle(\varphi_{\beta,\delta}^{\prime}-\varphi_{\beta,\delta})^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mu^{1/2}(v(\kappa))\mathrm{d}\kappa.

(4.42)

combing these two estimates gives

\displaystyle(\varphi_{\beta,\delta}^{\prime}-\varphi_{\beta,\delta})^{2}\lesssim\delta^{2}\min\{1,\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\}\int_{0}^{1}((\mu^{1/2})^{\prime}+\mu^{1/2}+\mu^{1/2}(v(\kappa)))\mathrm{d}\kappa.

(4.43)

By Lemma
2.2
and Lemma
3.6
,
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\lesssim\delta^{2}\int b^{s}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}\psi^{\eta}(|v-v_{*}|)\min\{1,\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}\eta^{\alpha}\int\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{\alpha+2s}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}\eta^{\alpha}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\alpha+2s},

which completes the proof of the lemma. ∎

We are now ready to prove the coercivity estimate of
\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}
for
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
by induction.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

In the proof,
0<\eta=\min\{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\}<1
is a fixed constant. Hence,
1\leq\eta^{\gamma}\leq\eta^{-5}
for
-5\leq\gamma\leq 0
. For brevity, set

\displaystyle J^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f):=4\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle,\quad\mathbb{A}(g,h):=(g_{*}h+gh_{*}-g^{\prime}_{*}h^{\prime}-g^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}),\quad\mathbb{F}(g,h):=\mathbb{A}^{2}(g,h).

With these notations, we have

J^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)=\int B^{s,\gamma,\eta}\mathbb{F}(\mu^{1/2},f)\mathrm{d}V.

We divide the proof into five steps.

Step 1: Localization of
\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)
.

By (
4.35
) and if
a\leq 0
, we get

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|^{-a}\leq\max\{1,2^{-a-1}\}\delta^{a}((\delta|v|)^{-a}+(\delta|v_{*}|)^{-a})\leq 2\max\{1,2^{-a-1}\}\delta^{a}U^{-a}_{\delta}(v)U^{-a}_{\delta}(v_{*}),

which gives

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|^{a}\geq C_{a}\delta^{-a}U^{a}_{\delta}(v)U^{a}_{\delta}(v_{*}),

where
C_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\min\{1,2^{a+1}\}
.
With
\gamma=\alpha+\beta,\gamma\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}U^{\beta}_{\delta}(U^{\beta}_{\delta})_{*}\mathbb{F}(\mu^{1/2},f)\mathrm{d}V.

By setting
h=U^{\beta/2}_{\delta},\phi=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}
and commuting the weight function
U^{\beta}_{\delta}(U^{\beta}_{\delta})_{*}
with
\mathbb{F}(\cdot,\cdot)
, we have

\displaystyle U^{\beta}_{\delta}(U^{\beta}_{\delta})_{*}\mathbb{F}(\mu^{1/2},f)

(4.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{2}_{*}h^{2}\mathbb{F}(\phi,f)=\left(hh_{*}\left(\phi_{*}f+\phi f_{*}\right)-hh_{*}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(hh_{*}\left(\phi_{*}f+\phi f_{*}\right)-h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)+\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(hh_{*}\left(\phi_{*}f+\phi f_{*}\right)-h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)\right)^{2}-\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{F}(h\phi,hf)-\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)^{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\mathbb{F}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)\mathrm{d}V

(4.45)

\displaystyle-C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

We further rewrite

\mathbb{F}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)

as
\mathbb{F}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)
plus some correction terms. That is,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{2}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)

(4.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{A}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)-\mathbb{A}\big((1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f\big)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{A}^{2}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)-\mathbb{A}^{2}\big((1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{F}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)-\mathbb{F}((1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f).

By symmetry and noting
\phi=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\left(\phi^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}+\phi^{\prime}f^{\prime}_{*}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}V\leq 4\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\mu_{*}f^{2}\mathrm{d}V.

(4.47)

By (
4.45
), (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\mathbb{F}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\mathbb{F}((1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle-4C_{\beta}\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\left(h^{\prime}h^{\prime}_{*}-hh_{*}\right)^{2}\mu_{*}f^{2}\mathrm{d}V

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}C_{\beta}J_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}-\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}-4C_{\beta}J_{3}^{\alpha,\beta}.

We always choose
\beta
in the range

-1\leq-s\leq\beta\leq 0
. It is straightforward to check that

C_{\beta}=\frac{1}{2}
. Noting that
J_{1}^{\alpha,\beta}=\delta^{-\beta}\mathcal{J}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq\frac{1}{8}\delta^{s}\mathcal{J}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)-\frac{1}{4}J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}-2J_{3}^{\alpha,\beta}.

(4.48)

Step 2: Upper bound of
J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}
.

For simplicity of notations, set
\varphi_{\beta,\delta}=(1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}},\psi_{\beta,\delta}=U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f
. Then

\displaystyle J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}=\delta^{-\beta}\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}\mathbb{F}(\varphi_{\beta,\delta},\varphi_{\beta,\delta})\mathrm{d}V\lesssim\delta^{-\beta}\mathcal{N}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(\varphi_{\beta,\delta},\varphi_{\beta,\delta})+\delta^{-\beta}\mathcal{N}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(\varphi_{\beta,\delta},\varphi_{\beta,\delta}).

(4.49)

By (
4.37
), for
a\leq 0
,

\displaystyle 0\leq 1-U^{a}_{\delta}(v)=U^{a}_{\delta}(0)-U^{a}_{\delta}(v)\lesssim|a|\delta|v|.

(4.50)

By (
4.50
), we have

\displaystyle(\varphi_{\beta,\delta}^{2})_{*}=((1-U^{\beta/2}_{\delta})\mu^{\frac{1}{2}})^{2}_{*}\lesssim\delta^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}.

(4.51)

From this and Prop.
3.2
, by using the fact that
\delta^{-\beta/2}U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}\in S^{\beta/2}_{1,0}
is a radial symbol of order
\beta/2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta^{-\beta}\mathcal{N}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(\varphi_{\beta,\delta},\varphi_{\beta,\delta})\lesssim\delta^{2}\delta^{-\beta}\mathcal{N}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(\mu^{1/4},\psi_{\beta,\delta})\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}\delta^{-\beta}|U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{s,\alpha/2}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.52)

By Lemma
4.10
, we then have

\displaystyle J_{2,2}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}\eta^{\alpha}\delta^{-\beta}\int(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)^{2}_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\alpha+2s}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}|W_{\gamma/2+s}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.53)

Plugging the estimates (
4.52
) and (
4.53
) into (
4.49
), we get

\displaystyle J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.54)

Step 3: Upper bound of
J_{2}^{\alpha,\beta}
.

Lemma
4.9
gives

\displaystyle J_{3}^{\alpha,\beta}=\int B^{s,\alpha,\eta}X(\beta,\delta)\mu_{*}f^{2}\mathrm{d}V\lesssim s^{-1}\delta^{2s}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}.

(4.55)

Step 4: The case
-s\leq\gamma<0
.
We take
\alpha=0,\beta=\gamma
.
Recall
\mathcal{J}^{s,\alpha,\eta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)=4\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\alpha,\eta}U^{\gamma/2}_{\delta}f,U^{\gamma/2}_{\delta}f\rangle
.
By Lemma
4.8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,0,\eta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)\geq 4\lambda_{0}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})U^{\gamma/2}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{s,0}\geq 4\lambda_{0}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})U^{\gamma/2}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}.

(4.56)

We claim that there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that if
0<\delta\leq\delta_{1}
, then for any
-5/2\leq a\leq 0
,

\displaystyle|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})U^{a}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}\geq\frac{1}{4}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s+a}}.

(4.57)

This yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,0,\eta}(U^{\beta/2}_{\delta}f)\geq\lambda_{0}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s+\gamma/2}}.

(4.58)

We now prove (
4.57
).
Note that

\displaystyle|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})(U^{a}_{\delta}f)|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}\geq\frac{1}{2}|U^{a}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}-|\mathbf{P}(U^{a}_{\delta}f)|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}.

Since
\delta\leq 1
and
a\leq 0
,
U^{a}_{\delta}\geq W_{a}
. Hence,

\displaystyle|U^{a}_{\delta}f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}\geq|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s+a}}.

(4.59)

We now estimate
|\mathbf{P}(U^{a}_{\delta}f)|_{L^{2}}
for
f\in\ker^{\perp}
.
Since

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(U^{a}_{\delta}f)=\sum_{i=1}^{5}e_{i}\int e_{i}U^{a}_{\delta}f\mathrm{d}v=\sum_{i=1}^{5}e_{i}\int e_{i}(U^{a}_{\delta}-1)f\mathrm{d}v,

then

\displaystyle\big|\int e_{i}(U^{a}_{\delta}-1)f\mathrm{d}v\big|\lesssim|a|\delta|\mu^{\frac{1}{8}}f|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathbf{P}(U^{a}_{\delta}f)|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s}}\lesssim a^{2}\delta^{2}|\mu^{\frac{1}{8}}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta^{2}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{s+a}}.

(4.60)

By combining the estimates (
4.59
) and (
4.60
)
and choosing
\delta_{1}
suitably small,
we obtain (
4.57
).

By plugging the estimates (
4.58
), (
4.54
), (
4.55
) into (
4.48
), for any
-s\leq\gamma\leq 0
and

0<\delta\leq\delta_{1}
, for some generic constants
0<C_{1}
and
1\leq C_{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq C_{1}\delta^{s}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}-C_{2}s^{-1}\delta^{2s}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.61)

It is straightforward to check from above that

C_{1}=\lambda_{0}/8
.
Recalling Theorem
4.1
,
for some generic constants
0<C_{3}\leq 1\leq C_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq C_{3}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}-C_{4}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}.

(4.62)

We can assume
\frac{C_{1}C_{3}}{2C_{4}C_{2}s^{-1}}\leq\delta_{1}^{s}
. Otherwise, we can take a larger
C_{4}
.

Then the combination
\eqref{known-estimate}\times C_{5}\delta^{2s}+\eqref{key-estimate-order-s-2s}
gives

\displaystyle(1+C_{5}\delta^{2s})\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq(C_{1}-C_{4}C_{5}\delta^{s})\delta^{s}|f|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}+(C_{3}C_{5}-C_{2}s^{-1})\delta^{2s}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.63)

We can then take
C_{5}
large enough such that
C_{3}C_{5}-C_{2}s^{-1}\geq C_{2}s^{-1}
, for example
C_{5}=2C_{2}s^{-1}/C_{3}\geq 2
.
And then we choose
\delta
small enough such that
C_{1}-C_{4}C_{5}\delta^{s}\geq 0
, for example
\delta^{s}=\frac{C_{1}}{C_{4}C_{5}}=\frac{C_{1}C_{3}}{2C_{4}C_{2}s^{-1}}\leq\delta_{1}^{s}
. Note that we can assume

C_{5}\delta^{2s}=\frac{C_{1}^{2}C_{3}}{2C_{4}^{2}C_{2}s^{-1}}\leq\delta_{1}^{s}\frac{C_{1}}{C_{4}}\leq 1
. Otherwise, we can take a larger
C_{4}
.
Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{2}s^{-1}\delta^{2s}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}=\frac{1}{2}C_{2}s^{-1}\left(\frac{C_{1}C_{3}}{2C_{2}s^{-1}C_{4}}\right)^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{C_{3}}{16(C_{2}s^{-1})^{1/2}C_{4}}\right)^{2}\lambda_{0}^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2}.

(4.64)

Recalling
\mathcal{J}^{s,\gamma,\eta}(f)=4\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle
, we get for
-s\leq\gamma\leq 0
that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\gamma,\eta}f,f\rangle\geq c_{s}\lambda_{0}^{2}|f|^{2}_{s,\gamma/2},

where

\displaystyle c_{s}=\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{C_{3}}{16C_{2}^{1/2}C_{4}}\right)^{2}s=2^{-11}C_{3}^{2}C_{2}^{-1}C_{4}^{-2}s.

Step 5: The case
-ks\leq\gamma<-(k-1)s
for
k\geq 2
.

In the previous step, starting from the
\gamma=0
case
by using Lemma
4.8
where the constant is
\lambda_{0}
,
to derive the
-s\leq\gamma<0
case, we have a new constant
c_{s}\lambda_{0}^{2}
.
For

-ks\leq\gamma<-(k-1)s
, we can choose

\alpha=-(k-1)s
and
\beta=\gamma+(k-1)s
to apply the result of
\langle\mathcal{L}^{s,\alpha,\eta}f,f\rangle
.
Note that the constants
C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}

are generaic with respect to
\alpha,\beta
satisfying
\alpha+\beta=\gamma,-s\leq\beta\leq 0,-5\leq\gamma\leq\alpha\leq 0
.

\lambda_{n}=c_{s}\lambda_{n-1}^{2}
implies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}=c_{s}^{2^{k}-1}\lambda_{0}^{2^{k}}.

For
-ks\leq\gamma<(k-1)s
, by induction
we will have
